- Manual QA: Spot-check sea-level and monitoring datasets against the official CSV inventory.

Live profile returned 9/9 priority datasets with `ok` status. Spot-checked sea-level anomalies and meteorological monitoring network contracts against the official inventory URLs and expected coverage.
- Attempts: 1
- Max attempts: 3

## TASK-003
- Phase: methodology
- Title: Implement Adaptation Gap Index baseline
- Depends on: TASK-002
- Assigned agent: Codex
- Contract refs: configs/gap_index.yml, context/ASSUMPTIONS.md
- Data refs: data/processed
- Scientific refs: context/DATA_CARD.md
- User value / decision value: Creates the central comparison layer for the atlas.
- Functional notes: Compute climate pressure, capacity, adaptation gap score, and missingness flags.
- Statistical notes: Use transparent percentile-rank normalization; do not impute primary score values.
- Edge cases: Geographies without capacity data should show insufficient-data state rather than a fake score.
- Files to create/modify: `analysis/features/gap_index.py`, `scripts/build_gap_index.py`, `artifacts/tables/*`, `context/docs/methodology.md`
- Artifacts to produce: index table, methodology note, score distribution figure if useful
- Acceptance criteria: Each published score traces back to included indicators and missingness flags.
- Verification commands: `python scripts/build_gap_index.py --config configs/gap_index.yml`
- Manual QA: Review top and bottom ranked geographies for obvious data artifacts.
- QA notes: Added tested baseline scoring helpers, implemented `scripts/build_gap_index.py`, generated `artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_index.csv`, generated `artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_indicator_trace.csv`, and wrote `artifacts/provenance/gap_index_summary.json`. Output includes 22 geographies, 22 scored geographies, 0 insufficient-data geographies, and 182 indicator trace rows. Manual QA reviewed top and bottom ranked geographies; PN, NR, AS, WF, and TV currently rank highest, while PF, FJ, NC, GU, and PG rank lowest under the draft method.
- Attempts: 1
- Max attempts: 3
- Attempt log: Built latest-observation percentile-rank baseline with anomaly magnitude scoring, no imputation, required climate/adaptation pillars, and trace rows for score auditability.
- Status: done

## TASK-004
- Phase: modeling
- Title: Build Adaptation Gap Outlook baseline
- Depends on: TASK-003
- Assigned agent: Codex
- Contract refs: configs/outlook.yml, context/MODEL_CARD.md
- Data refs: data/processed
- Scientific refs: context/ASSUMPTIONS.md, context/EXPERIMENTS.md
- User value / decision value: Adds a cautious future-facing layer if the data supports it.
- Functional notes: Estimate climate-signal trends and combine with capacity scenarios.
- Statistical notes: Use time-aware evaluation and compare against a naive baseline.
- Edge cases: Sparse time series should be excluded from projections and labeled in the app.
- Files to create/modify: `analysis/modeling/outlook.py`, `analysis/evaluation/*`, `scripts/run_outlook.py`, `artifacts/logs/runs/*`, `context/MODEL_CARD.md`, `context/EXPERIMENTS.md`
- Artifacts to produce: run bundle, metrics, projection table, model card update
- Acceptance criteria: Outlook results include uncertainty/caveat fields and are not described as operational predictions.
- Verification commands: `python scripts/run_outlook.py --config configs/outlook.yml`
- Manual QA: Compare outlook rankings to current gap rankings and investigate surprises.
- QA notes: Added tested outlook trend helpers, implemented `scripts/run_outlook.py`, generated `artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_outlook.csv`, generated `artifacts/tables/climate_trend_diagnostics.csv`, wrote `artifacts/provenance/outlook_summary.json`, and produced a local ignored run bundle under `artifacts/logs/runs/task-004-outlook-baseline/`. Output includes 86 trend series, 88 projection rows, 86 backtested series, 39 series where linear trend beats naive, mean linear holdout MAE 1.7731, and mean naive holdout MAE 2.9953. Decision: methodology-ready and app-optional, not a primary prediction layer.
- Attempts: 1
- Max attempts: 3
- Attempt log: Built transparent trend stress-test baseline with 2030/2050 horizons, capacity-flat and gradual-improvement scenarios, residual diagnostics, holdout comparisons, and row-level caveats.
- Status: done

## TASK-019
- Phase: analysis
- Title: Add evidence fingerprint divergence analysis
- Depends on: TASK-012, TASK-013, TASK-014, TASK-018
- Assigned agent: Codex data agent
- Contract refs: context/INFORMATION_DIVERGENCE_PLAN.md, context/ANALYSIS_BACKLOG.md, context/plans/evidence-fingerprint-divergence-plan.md
- Data refs: artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_indicator_trace.csv, artifacts/tables/eda_country_drivers.csv, artifacts/tables/eda_rank_volatility.csv, artifacts/tables/eda_monitoring_gap.csv
- Scientific refs: context/DATA_CARD.md, context/ASSUMPTIONS.md, context/STORY_BRIEF.md
- User value / decision value: Lets the atlas compare the shape of official-data evidence profiles, not only gap-score magnitude.
- Functional notes: Build normalized pressure, capacity, data-visibility, and combined evidence vectors; compute pairwise Jensen-Shannon divergence; optionally compute KL only for internal diagnostics after smoothing review.
- Statistical notes: Treat JSD as an exploratory similarity metric over official-data profiles. Do not present it as causal clustering, shared vulnerability, shared policy need, or a replacement score.
- Edge cases: Sparse/missing profile components can create artificial similarity; missingness must be encoded and caveated rather than silently smoothed away.
- Files to create/modify: `analysis/eda/divergence.py`, `scripts/run_eda.py`, `tests/analysis/test_divergence.py`, `artifacts/tables/eda_evidence_fingerprints.csv`, `artifacts/tables/eda_pairwise_jsd.csv`, `artifacts/tables/eda_similarity_neighbors.csv`, `artifacts/provenance/divergence_summary.json`, `context/ANALYSIS_BRIEF.md`, `context/DESIGN_BRIEF.md`
- Artifacts to produce: evidence-fingerprint table, pairwise JSD matrix/table, nearest-neighbor table, provenance summary, app-layer recommendation.
- Acceptance criteria: Pairwise JSD is symmetric and bounded; vector components are documented and traceable; missingness treatment is explicit; nearest-neighbor outputs include caveat fields; no public-facing output requires interpreting KL.
- Verification commands: `python scripts/run_eda.py --config configs/eda.yml`; `python -m unittest tests.analysis.test_divergence -v`; `python scripts/check_secrets.py`; `python scripts/validate_task_statuses.py`
- Manual QA: Inspect nearest-neighbor pairs for primary exemplars such as NR, TV, PN, AS, WF, and MH; verify the layer explains "similar score, different profile" without creating a leaderboard.
- QA notes: Implemented `analysis/eda/divergence.py`, integrated the EDA runner, generated 22 evidence fingerprints, 231 unordered pairwise JSD rows, 66 nearest-neighbor rows, and `artifacts/provenance/divergence_summary.json`. Public metric is base-2 Jensen-Shannon divergence over normalized official-data-derived profiles; KL is not required for public interpretation. Exemplar neighbors for NR, TV, PN, AS, WF, and MH are recorded in provenance. The layer is analysis-ready but not app-wired, and must stay selected-geography anchored if it ships.
- Attempts: 1
- Max attempts: 3
- Attempt log: Codex data-agent implementation, Codex review, context cleanup, and validation.
- Status: done

Ran land-vs-centroid data profiling first: all 22 centroids have Natural Earth land within 3 degrees (min Niue 1 feature at 0.2x0.2 deg, max PNG 23 features over ~6 deg), and close neighbors overlap (AS's 3-degree neighborhood contains WS islands), so radius-lit land would misattribute neighbors' islands. Design directions constrained accordingly; owner direction requested before prototyping. Owner chose the viewfinder-frame-plus-land-tick treatment from three options; design approved before code. Spec/plan recorded in context/plans/task-035-island-anchor-viewfinder-plan.md. Built TDD-first: `nearestLandCenter` and `viewfinderGeometry` are pure functions in atlasMapModel.ts with red-then-green Vitest coverage pinning the AS-resolves-to-Tutuila-not-Samoa misattribution case, the 45px/17%-viewport window clamps, and tick suppression when land sits under the point (Niue). AtlasMap renders the frame, tick, and "map area, not territory" note in the SVG overlay from the tested geometry; the old point-level selection bracket is replaced; priority halos and monitoring rings untouched; land never takes the score ramp. DESIGN_BRIEF selection-state section updated. Codex geometry decision: no boundary source required for this treatment; territorial-extent highlighting remains a future reviewed-source task; DATA_CARD/methodology untouched because geometry semantics did not change. Fable evidence passed, then Codex QA re-ran verification and accepted the task.

## TASK-038
- Phase: map-design
- Title: Add selection island halo over nearest-centroid land grouping
- Depends on: TASK-035
- Assigned agent: Fable Builder, Codex geometry QA
- Contract refs: context/DESIGN_BRIEF.md, context/plans/task-035-island-anchor-viewfinder-plan.md, context/DATA_CARD.md

- User value / decision value: Owner review of TASK-035 found the viewfinder too quiet; the selected place's island shapes should visibly light up while staying honest about boundaries.
- Functional notes: Group each Natural Earth land polygon to its nearest scored centroid within a cutoff and halo the selected geography's island outlines via MapLibre line layers. Score ramp stays off land; grouping is a visual highlight aid, not a boundary source.
- Statistical notes: Nearest-centroid grouping resolves close-neighbor adjacency (AS anchors Tutuila, not Samoa's islands); the 3.5-degree cutoff leaves far context land and the disputed Matthew & Hunter islands unassigned by construction. Score-colored island polygons remain gated on a reviewed boundary-source task.
- Edge cases: Disputed features must never inherit an anchor; unassigned land renders as plain context; halo must never use the score ramp; filter must clear when selection clears.
- Files to create/modify: `app/src/components/map/AtlasMap.tsx`, `app/src/components/map/atlasMapModel.ts`, `app/src/components/map/atlasMapModel.test.ts`, `context/DESIGN_BRIEF.md`, `context/plans/task-035-island-anchor-viewfinder-plan.md`, `context/TASKS.md`, `context/logs/Progress Log.md`
- Artifacts to produce: tested land-anchor grouping, halo layers, updated design-brief note, Codex geometry QA review of grouping rule and exclusions.
- Acceptance criteria: Selected geography's island outlines glow without implying boundaries or scores on land; disputed and far land stays unassigned; tests and build pass; owner visual review in a clean browser.
- Verification commands: `npm --prefix app run test`; `npm --prefix app run build`; `python scripts/validate_task_statuses.py`; `python scripts/check_secrets.py`; `git diff --check`
- Manual QA: Owner local run: select NR, AS, FJ, PF; confirm island glow, no neighbor bleed, no halo when nothing is selected; Codex reviews the grouping rule and Matthew & Hunter exclusion.
- QA notes: Codex quick QA accepted the selection island halo. Source review confirmed `assignLandAnchors` tags Natural Earth land by nearest scored centroid only for selection highlighting, leaves far/disputed land unassigned via the 3.5-degree cutoff, does not mutate input features, and never colors land with the score ramp. MapLibre halo layers are white line layers filtered to the selected code and clear to a no-match filter when no selection exists. The on-map note says "islands grouped by distance, not boundaries", preserving the non-boundary caveat. Fresh verification passed: `npm --prefix app run test` (2 files, 9 tests), `npm --prefix app run build`, `python scripts/validate_task_statuses.py`, `python scripts/check_secrets.py`, and `git diff --check`. Runtime owner visual review remains the final taste gate, but geometry/source QA is accepted.
- Attempts: 1
- Max attempts: 3
- Attempt log: 2026-07-04: Owner directed this addition during TASK-035 visual review (Codex accepted and closed TASK-035's viewfinder in parallel, so the halo lands as its own task). Built same-session, TDD-first: `assignLandAnchors(land, geos, maxDeg=3.5)` in atlasMapModel.ts with red-then-green tests pinning AS-not-WS anchoring, cutoff nulls, disputed Matthew & Hunter nulls by construction, and input immutability. AtlasMap memoizes the tagged land collection, feeds it to the existing land source, adds two line layers (blurred white glow plus crisp outline, never the score ramp) filtered to the selected geo code, and updates the filter on selection change; the viewfinder whisper note now reads "islands grouped by distance, not boundaries". DESIGN_BRIEF selection-state note extended; attempt-2 section appended to the TASK-035 plan file. Fable evidence passed, then Codex QA re-ran verification and accepted the task.

## TASK-068
- Phase: analysis
- Title: Test regional cross-currents and evidence visibility with extended EDA
- Depends on: TASK-067
- Assigned agent: Codex Builder; independent scientific reviewer; owner QA
- Contract refs: context/PROBLEM.md, context/ANALYSIS_BACKLOG.md, configs/eda.yml
- Data refs: data/processed/official_observations.csv, data/processed/app/geographies.json, data/processed/app/pacific_land_context.geojson, artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_index.csv, artifacts/tables/adaptation_gap_indicator_trace.csv, artifacts/tables/eda_coverage_by_geography.csv, artifacts/tables/eda_monitoring_gap.csv
- Scientific refs: context/ANALYSIS_BRIEF.md, context/DATA_CARD.md, context/EXPERIMENTS.md
- User value / decision value: Determines whether the approved regional two-act direction is supported across the Pacific rather than by a few attractive examples, while preserving unequal evidence visibility as a visible limit instead of mislabeling it as emergency preparedness.
- Functional notes: Extend the script-first EDA with six deterministic regional research plates: within-indicator distributions; all-geography water/renewable cross-currents; a cluster-ordered measured-condition heatmap; a separately ordered evidence-visibility heatmap; a pairwise relationship matrix with overlap and dependency warnings; and centroid-map small multiples for conditions and visibility. Keep all available geographies visible. Named places may be annotated only after the full distribution proves they are illustrative. Produce a file-based run bundle under `artifacts/logs/runs/<run_id>/`. Do not modify the app, public app data, title, storyboard, score, or final scene copy.
- Statistical notes: Keep raw values, units, years, denominators, and missingness traceable. Standardize only as within-indicator percentiles for research ordering; never average those percentiles into a score. Use Spearman relationships with pairwise `n`, latest-year range, and derived-field dependency flags; withhold underpowered cells. Use hierarchical ordering only as an exploratory heatmap seriation. Condition and visibility matrices must be constructed and ordered separately, without imputation. Run leave-one-indicator sensitivity; if ordering or groups are unstable, record rejection rather than name clusters. Treat monitoring, power, and fisheries fields as visible proxies, not complete readiness. Flag circular comparisons where a derived score already contains the compared input.
- Edge cases: Only 22 geographies exist; water/renewable overlap is 19; direct loss has 39 records across 12 geographies and is visibility-only; land-cover direction is unresolved; population rows are projections/estimates rather than realized change or size; latest years differ; centroid fallback is not boundary geometry; a visually strong cluster may be driven by missingness, duplicated inputs, or one indicator.
- Files to create/modify: pyproject.toml; configs/eda.yml; create analysis/eda/regional_patterns.py; create analysis/eda/regional_figures.py; scripts/run_eda.py; scripts/check_required_artifacts.py; create tests/analysis/test_eda_regional_patterns.py; artifacts/tables/eda_regional_feature_matrix.csv; artifacts/tables/eda_regional_distribution_summary.csv; artifacts/tables/eda_regional_crosscurrents.csv; artifacts/tables/eda_regional_pairwise_relationships.csv; artifacts/tables/eda_regional_cluster_stability.csv; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_distributions.png; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_crosscurrents.png; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_condition_heatmap.png; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_visibility_heatmap.png; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_relationships.png; artifacts/figures/eda_regional_maps.png; one deterministic `artifacts/logs/runs/<run_id>/` bundle; artifacts/provenance/eda_summary.json; existing context and logs after review
- Interfaces: `build_regional_tables(observations, index, indicator_trace, coverage, monitoring_gap) -> dict[str, pandas.DataFrame]` owns reviewed transforms and table contracts. `render_regional_research_atlas(tables, geographies, land_context, output_dir) -> dict[str, pathlib.Path]` renders only from those tables. The runner owns file I/O, deterministic output order, provenance, and the seven-file run bundle; figure code must not recompute headline metrics from hidden constants.
- Implementation sequence: Write RED tests for regional coverage, missingness preservation, cross-current quadrants, pairwise overlap/dependency flags, separate condition/visibility ordering, and leave-one-indicator stability; implement the smallest table transforms; render and inspect the six plates; integrate tables/figures/run bundle into the existing runner and required-artifact gate; rerun determinism, full tests, scientific review, and full-size manual QA before owner review.
- Artifacts to produce: five reviewed regional evidence tables, six full-size static research figures, refreshed EDA provenance, and one valid file-based run bundle containing `meta.json`, `config.yaml`, `seeds.json`, `inputs.json`, `outputs.json`, `metrics.json`, and `notes.md`
- Acceptance criteria: Every plate answers one declared analytical question and visibly states units, time basis, geography/sample count, source, and primary caveat; all 22 geographies remain represented or explicitly marked missing; water/renewable examples are evaluated against the 19-place overlap; measured-condition and evidence-visibility ordering are separate by construction; correlations expose `n`, years, and circular/derived dependencies; cluster ordering is deterministic and either survives the declared sensitivity check or is explicitly rejected; no value is imputed and no new composite, readiness class, causal claim, polygon claim, app change, or final narrative is produced; two consecutive runs reproduce table/figure/provenance hashes and a valid run bundle.

- Manual QA: Open all six figures at full size; trace at least one typical, one extreme, and one missing geography through source rows, table cells, and plotted marks; confirm dependency warnings prevent circular capacity/monitoring interpretation; verify maps use equal-presence centroid marks and do not imply reviewed boundaries; challenge every proposed regional takeaway against the complete distribution rather than the highlighted labels.
- QA notes: Builder RED/GREEN covered all-place coverage, missingness preservation, separate condition/visibility construction, 19-place cross-current quadrants, pairwise overlap/dependency warnings, NaN-aware ordering sensitivity, run-bundle contracts, denominator/source-row-hash traceability, and deterministic crosscurrent label offsets. Five tables and six full-size plates retain all 22 geographies or explicit missing marks. Typical Tonga, extreme Papua New Guinea, and missing-water Guam were traced from processed source rows through table cells to plotted marks; equal-size centroid maps use Natural Earth only as quiet context. The 19 complete endpoint comparisons split 7/6/3/3; 36 relationships include four direct/transitive derived-score warnings. Reviewer follow-up confirmed gap/monitoring is indirectly dependent through capacity; its rho -0.7927, n=18 table cell and both matrix annotations now carry the warning/dagger. ## TASK-090

- Functional notes: This is a concept-only task with no production-code authority. Produce three controlled directions against the same premise, visibility, and Explorer frames: restrained hydrographic chart furniture; atmospheric ocean depth/light; and a hybrid that evolves the existing dark-ocean atlas. Each direction must state what is decorative, what encodes data, how it behaves in grayscale and reduced motion, and what current identity elements remain. Compare any proposed glass, brass, cream, neatline, bathymetric, ripple, or sounding treatment instead of declaring it approved. Reuse current screenshots and generated facts; no new map source, renderer, dependency, or app branch. Owner selects, combines, or rejects the directions before any implementation tasks are created.
- Statistical notes: Reporting status and dataset presence may not be encoded as live water, dead water, wave activity, calmness, depth, current, weather, sonar contact, or environmental condition. Coverage numerals, if auditioned, must be explicitly N-of-14 record visibility and cannot appear in score layers. No cultural navigation, weaving, tattoo, canoe, or Indigenous motif may be used without Pacific co-design and provenance.
- Edge cases: Decorative linework mistaken for boundaries or measured bathymetry; sonar or military associations; data absence mistaken for physical absence; motion-sensitive users; grayscale; dense small-island regions; mobile portrait/landscape; visual continuity with selected-place panels and controls.

## TASK-101
- Phase: place-context-correctness
- Title: Correct the reader-facing political-status line
- Depends on: TASK-088
- Assigned agent: Content/data-adapter Builder; independent narrative/ethics Checker

- User value / decision value: Stops the interface from publishing the project's own editorial-review state as a hedge on each place's political status, and attributes that status instead.
- Functional notes: `statusLabel` in app/src/lib/atlasData.ts appends " (wording in review)" whenever `context_quality` contains `needs_review` or the status string contains `territory`, so 19 of 22 places render a reader-facing workflow marker; `titleCase` additionally capitalizes function words. Removing that marker is necessary but is not permission to publish the underlying wording without review. First verify all 22 political-status strings against current authoritative public sources, paying particular attention to NC, PF, WF, CK, NU, TK, and the coarse sovereign-state rows; update data/external/geography_context.csv and artifacts/provenance/geography_context_sources.json where the review changes a label, source, date, or caveat; then rebuild generated/public geography data. Store the reviewed public casing in the source data, return it verbatim from the adapter, delete both suffix branches and `titleCase`, and use "Political status unavailable" only as the defensive missing-value fallback. Add the exact uniform line "Political status is descriptive context from cited public sources; it is not used in any score." with the existing `.panel__fineprint` class, and add political-status context to the existing Methods source list so "cited" is true in the public interface. Do not build a function-word stoplist or add CSS.
- Statistical notes: `context_quality` is a pipeline workflow field, not a statement about a place; the current provenance explicitly says several labels still require publication review. The app must neither expose that workflow marker nor silently convert an unreviewed status into an unqualified claim. Political status stays descriptive context that feeds no score, and no place may be singled out by hedging.
- Edge cases: All 22 records including sovereign states (FJ, NR, TV, WS, TO, VU, PG, SB, KI), free-association states (CK, NU), the four `territory` substring matches (AS, GU, MP, PN), the sensitive French statuses (NC, PF, WF), TK, and the three records that already shipped unqualified (FM, MH, PW); missing-status defensive branch; 375px wrapping.
- Test cases: Assert the adapted payload contains no "wording in review" (mirroring the existing "Review wording before publication" guard), pin the reviewed exact strings for FJ/TK/CK/MP/PN, assert the uniform provenance sentence renders, and prove political-status context is named in Methods.

## TASK-102
- Phase: map-failure-resilience
- Title: Degrade gracefully when the map surface cannot initialize
- Depends on: TASK-093
- Assigned agent: Map/resilience Builder; independent accessibility/failure-state Checker
- Contract refs: context/ARTISTIC_REDESIGN_BRIEF.md identity boundary, context/DESIGN_BRIEF.md, TASK-093
- Data refs: existing generated app data; no data change
- Scientific refs: context/STORY_BRIEF.md, context/DATA_CARD.md
- User value / decision value: Prevents a blank application on a reader device that cannot provide WebGL, and stops the interface describing evidence it did not render.
- Functional notes: MapLibre 4.7.1 throws "Failed to initialize WebGL" synchronously inside the `Map` constructor, which runs in the mount effect of app/src/components/map/useAtlasMap.ts with no error boundary, so the whole tree unmounts. Add `mapError` state and wrap construction/setup in try/catch, leaving `mapRef.current` null on synchronous failure. Do not gate fatal startup errors through the React `mapReady` value captured by the mount effect: that closure remains false. Instead use a mount-local loaded flag or remove the startup-error listener immediately after `load`, keep a separate `webglcontextlost` listener, and route handler and effect cleanup through one idempotent teardown so a failed or removed map cannot be removed twice under StrictMode. Add a pure `mapStatusDescription` helper to atlasMapModel.ts as the testable seam. In the error state render neither the map container nor MapOverlay, and render a notice reusing the existing `.app-state` block scoped inside `.map-canvas` so panel, controls, story, and Methods stay usable. Leave the land-context catch alone. Do not touch App.tsx. No new dependency, renderer, or CSS.
- Statistical notes: The failure notice makes no claim about places, evidence, or conditions. The screen-reader map description must stop asserting 22 presence marks and Natural Earth land context when neither is rendered.
- Edge cases: WebGL blocklisted or unavailable at construction; hardware acceleration off; mid-session context loss; style/source load failure with a live context; guided and Explore modes; 1440x900 and 390x844; StrictMode double-invoked effects; routine non-fatal MapLibre tile warnings must not trigger the notice.
- Test cases: Unit-test `mapStatusDescription` in normal and error states; add the smallest source/lifecycle contract that pins constructor protection, startup-listener removal after load, context-loss handling, and idempotent teardown without asserting incidental whitespace.
- Files to create/modify: app/src/components/map/useAtlasMap.ts; app/src/components/map/atlasMapModel.ts; app/src/components/map/AtlasMap.tsx; app/src/components/map/atlasMapModel.test.ts; app/src/components/map/mapPresentation.test.tsx; context/TASKS.md; context/logs/Progress Log.md
- Artifacts to produce: WebGL-unavailable, normal, and mid-session context-loss captures plus additive failure-state assertions
- Acceptance criteria: With WebGL unavailable the page is not blank, the notice renders inside the map region, panel/controls/Methods remain operable, and the accessible description claims no unrendered evidence; normal load still renders 22 marks and 22 hit targets with `.map-note` unchanged; mid-session context loss transitions without an uncaught error; all repository gates pass.
- Verification commands: npm --prefix app run test; npm --prefix app run build; python scripts/check_app_bundle_budget.py; python scripts/validate_task_statuses.py; python scripts/check_secrets.py; git diff --check
- Manual QA: Quiet/headless runs with WebGL disabled, with a normal context, and with a forced `WEBGL_lose_context`, in both modes at both viewports.
- Attempt 3 QA notes: Strict RED failed exactly 1/7 focused presentation checks because the desktop panel-aware failure-state rule was absent. One existing-seam CSS rule reserves `var(--panel-w)` only at `min-width: 881px`; mutating `margin-right` to `margin-left` reproduced the focused failure before restoration returned 7/7 GREEN. Quiet headless Chromium passed 37/37 checks: selected NR failure states have zero heading/panel overlap and center within the unobscured map region at 881, 1024, 1229, 1230, and 1440 widths; no-panel 1024/1440 states remain full-canvas centered; 390x844 and 844x390 retain full-width alert and bottom-sheet geometry; all cases keep the heading in-view, panel controls usable, and horizontal/runtime/request errors at zero. The corrected 1024x768 frame was visually inspected under `artifacts/design/task-102/`. Full frontend passed 135/135, production build passed, bundle diagnostics passed at 1,042,934-byte JS and 95,474-byte CSS, and status, secrets, and whitespace gates passed.
- QA notes: Strict RED first produced five focused failures for the missing map-status seam, unprotected constructor/startup lifecycle, absent context-loss handling, non-idempotent cleanup, and failure renderer; GREEN passed 26/26 focused checks. Lifecycle review then reproduced re-entrant setup, uncancelled projection work, late map-ref retirement, retired land-listener cleanup, and delayed land-sync risks; one additive RED failed 1/9 before the same idempotent teardown gained retirement guards. Quiet raw-CDP QA used the cached Chromium headless shell with temporary `/tmp` profiles, loopback-only host resolution, and background/update/sync disabled because the browser-use wrapper cannot write its Brave profile inside the sandbox. The final 12-case matrix covered normal WebGL, forced `WEBGL_lose_context`, and GPU/software-rasterizer-disabled construction in guided/Explore at 1440x900 and 390x844. Normal cases render 22 marks and 22 targets; all eight failure cases render zero MapLibre containers, overlays, marks, and targets plus the in-region alert; guided story/Methods and Explore controls/Nauru panel/Methods remain present; all cases have zero horizontal overflow and no runtime exception. Visual inspection caught the verbose mobile notice overlapping guided copy; a final RED/GREEN moved its full description to the existing screen-reader-only class and recapture confirmed separation without CSS. Two initial SwiftShader ReadPixels performance warnings were browser-renderer diagnostics; failure cases emitted no runtime event. Attempt 2 replaced source-token lifecycle checks with a controllable hook/MapLibre harness. Its RED reproduced synchronous removal during setup, load, and context-loss dispatch plus stale projection access and startup-specific copy; a separate cleanup-reporting RED pinned nonfatal development diagnostics. GREEN now retires the public ref immediately, defers destructive event teardown, stops each startup helper before later operations/listeners, preserves post-load warnings as nonfatal, prevents context-loss default handling, removes exactly once, uses neutral visible copy, and reports rare removal failure only in development. Animating `right` across this breakpoint is wrong by construction because `top`, `bottom`, `width`, `height`, and `--panel-w` all change discontinuously at the same moment. Reproduce and confirm the cause first, then move the transition and the panel-open offset into the existing `@media (min-width: 881px)` block and delete the redundant mobile override. Keep `.dock--controls` in the reduced-motion list and preserve the TASK-094 cascade order. Delete the unreferenced `--ocean-2` and `--graticule` tokens because they are dead, not because a contest rule requires byte funding. Reorder and re-comment nothing else in base.css.
- Statistical notes: No data, claim, evidence, mark, or copy change.
- Edge cases: Live 1440 to 375 resize and back with a panel open; tablet rotation 768x1024 to 1024x768; the max-width 880px landscape block that also sets dock geometry; reduced motion in both directions; fresh-load baselines at 375px and 1440px; panel closed as well as open.
- Test cases: Assert `.dock--controls` carries no `transition` outside `@media (min-width: 881px)` and that exactly one `.atlas-shell--panel .dock--controls` declaration exists, inside that block; do not disturb the pinned source-order or comment-delimiter assertions.

This expected relocation/deletion should naturally reduce CSS, but a measured increase is reviewed on its merits rather than treated as a Challenge violation.
